# MultiLLM gateway gap analysis

A study of the 1,185 GitHub repositories tagged `llm-gateway`, taken on 2026-10-08, compared against MultiLLM at `origin/main` 54903f2. Third-party references link to the exact commit that was read. MultiLLM paths and line numbers refer to that commit; locate code by function name when they drift. Sections 6 and 7 are the build plan for the first two waves.

## 1. Method and coverage

This is an offline synthesis of the supplied study snapshot, not a live survey. Third-party source and README files were read as evidence only; nothing from them was executed or imported. No packages, browser sessions, network calls, repository source changes, or Git mutations were used.

The topic universe contains **1,185 repositories**: 118 with at least 20 stars, 523 with 1–19 stars, and 544 with zero stars. The delivered findings cover **592 distinct repositories**, leaving **593 without per-repository findings**. Thus adoption below is observed incidence among the delivered records, not a prevalence estimate for all 1,185 repositories.

| Research tier | Universe repositories | Repositories with findings | Feature records | Catalogued implemented | README/claimed |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| A01–A10: at least 20 stars | 118 | 109 | 2,609 | 1,907 | 702 |
| T1–T3: 1–19 stars | 523 | 401 | 1,203 | 0 | 1,203 |
| Z1–Z3: zero stars | 544 | 82 | 278 | 0 | 278 |
| Total | 1,185 | 592 | 4,090 | 1,907 | 2,183 |

There are **3,402 category-qualified raw feature IDs**, representing 3,374 bare ID strings: 28 strings recur in different categories. They map to **217 canonical capability clusters**. Appendix B preserves every category/ID assignment. Clustering combines ordered semantic rules, explicit overrides for distinctive mechanisms, and manual review of the high-priority clusters. Broad clusters describe capability families; their adoption count does not prove that every member implements the exact proposed mechanism. For example, generic sticky routing contributes to the affinity family, but does not establish prompt-cache-aware credential affinity.

The baseline contains 261 records: 43 shipped, 210 partial, and eight rejected. A baseline label is a starting point, not a verdict. Top-40 clusters were independently searched with case-insensitive `rg` over explicit first-party paths and relevant matched code was read. Absence searches, paths, and inspected incidental matches are in Appendix C; positive citations identify the existing boundary that remains incomplete. The complete assessment in Appendix A distinguishes this verification from baseline-only assessments of lower-ranked clusters.

The main/origin-main trees inspected were identical; the listed unmerged-feature capabilities are treated as already present. No feature is proposed merely because a historical branch name exists. Third-party references are commit-pinned links; MultiLLM paths are relative to the repository. Line numbers refer to this snapshot and should be re-located by named function when an implementation worktree starts.

**Status rules:** `has` means the useful capability for this operator is already present, including Worker-to-Flask forwarding where that endpoint uses the Container. `partial` names a concrete missing boundary rather than declaring the entire feature absent. `missing` means no relevant local implementation was found for a ranked cluster, or no equivalent baseline record for an unranked cluster; unranked absence is explicitly provisional. `rejected` means incompatible with the assessment policy, not absent by accident.

Important baseline corrections: roleplay already validates early stream output; the gap is managed automatic Chat streams. Cost accounting already stores unknown prices as null and records estimate/usage basis; the gap is incomplete usage fields, cache buckets and direct-edge coverage. Chat caching already exists and is principal-scoped; the gap is policy revision isolation and shared edge eligibility. Native Responses forwarding exists; hosted continuation ownership does not. The secret firewall is not a general PII classifier, notwithstanding a baseline `pii_redaction` label. Protocol conversion is allowed when explicit; only unconditional rewriting is rejected.

**Priority model (0–100):** adoption 20%, operator value 45%, effort 15%, Worker fit 20%. Distinct repositories are deduplicated case-insensitively across researchers. Star sums count each repository once per cluster. Claimed-only repositories contribute one-quarter of their stars to the adoption score; implemented repositories contribute full stars. With N repositories, W confidence-adjusted stars, and M named major gateways:

`A = 2 min(log(1+N)/log(61),1) + 2 min(log(1+W)/log(300001),1) + min(M/5,1)`

`P = 100 × (0.20 A/5 + 0.45 value/5 + 0.15 ease/5 + 0.20 fit/5) − conflict penalty`

Value and fit are 1–5. Effort: S under one day (ease 5), M one–three days (3.5), L more (1.5). Scores are engineering judgments, not measured benchmarks. Major gateways are LiteLLM, OmniRoute, Portkey, Bifrost, Kong, 9router, GoModel, ThinkWatch/ThinkWatch Lite and Plano. Rejected capabilities receive a policy exclusion rather than a build ranking. Lower-ranked family extensions have low incremental value because current capabilities already meet the main use case; their proposed effort is conservative until separately scoped.

Fit rewards bounded request work and existing D1/DO/R2 storage. This checkout explicitly uses D1 `INTELLIGENCE_DB` with `intelligence-migrations/` (`wrangler.jsonc:40–45`) and roleplay/container DO bindings (`wrangler.jsonc:48–57`). Do not assume a generic KV, Redis, Queue or Workflow binding exists merely because Cloudflare offers it. All six first-wave briefs need no new schema or binding.

## 2. Ranked gaps: top 40

N = distinct repositories; I = repositories with at least one catalogued implemented record; stars = unadjusted sum. Notable names show family adoption, not proof of the exact extension. A dash means none of the named majors, not no reference code. V and F are value and Worker fit out of five. Runtime `both` may mean Flask logic reached through the Worker Container; each Wave 1 brief specifies this boundary. Scores use the concrete opt-in, fidelity-preserving scope, without a policy conflict penalty.

Do not cryptographically certify estimates as provider-billed facts; unknown fields must survive signing. |

No tail README claim was treated as operational proof. Synthetic capacity fixtures, split-SSE canaries and offline conformance tests are useful testing techniques, not permission to alter real prompts or synthesize successful responses. Repeated compression, shadow promotion, memory and tool repair claims do not create new gaps where MultiLLM already implements the underlying mechanism.

## 4. Already covered

MultiLLM is already at or above the field on several combinations important to this operator:

- **Coding protocols and fidelity:** unified Chat, Responses and Messages plus explicit bridges (`routes/unified.py:853,978,991`), token-count estimation (`routes/unified_messages.py:57`), native provider routing and raw transport bypass (`services/proxy_service.py:1298`). Generic “add Responses/Anthropic support” proposals duplicate shipped work.
- **Managed fallback and operator control:** stored automatic routes and intelligence policies (`services/auto_route_service.py:457`, `services/intelligence_policy.py:207`), capability eligibility, operator-visible health, bounded transport retry and circuits. The next work is precision and safety around these mechanisms, not more strategy names.
- **Roleplay memory and generation recovery:** protected directives, compaction, persistence, continuation, validation and idempotent turn state (`worker/roleplay/endpoint.mjs:325,350,534,582`, `worker/roleplay/stream-validation-gate.mjs:77`). Candidate refitting extends this system rather than replacing it.
- **Evaluation controls:** comparison scenes, judges, cascade checks, shadow sampling, paired league scores and reviewable policy proposals (`services/comparison_lab.py:8`, `services/cascade_checks.py:58`, `services/shadow_eval_league.py:16`). “Learned routing” and “LLM-as-judge” claims alone are not missing features.
- **Prompt caching and explicit repair:** native prompt-cache controls already exist (`worker/roleplay/prompt-cache.mjs:1`), Chat cache is principal-scoped (`routes/chat_cache.py:118`), and tool repair is explicit (`routes/tool_repair.py:1`). Preserve fidelity defaults.
- **Knowledge/MCP beyond a basic proxy:** curated provider tools, narrowed discovery, approved skill search, evidence caching and handoffs (`routes/knowledge_mcp.py:60,89`, `worker/knowledge/cache.mjs:9`, `worker/knowledge/skills-index.mjs:64`, `worker/knowledge/handoffs.mjs:4`). Additional MCP version/permission mechanisms are extensions, not absent MCP support.
- **Media, accounting and operating surfaces:** image/video/audio and custom media batches, content-free usage rows with explicit price uncertainty (`services/request_accounting.py:393`), virtual-key restrictions (`services/key_controls.py:202`), secret firewall, deployment fingerprints and Container-sleep-safe public health (`worker/status-page.mjs:151`). A larger competitor's billing organization model adds little value here.

These are useful-coverage judgments; they do not imply every third-party variant or every provider is supported. The exact cluster-level disposition is retained in Appendix A.

## 5. Not recommended

| Capability or proposal | Decision and reason |
| --- | --- |
| Wholesale round-robin/weighted/least-cost/semantic/ML routing catalog | Rejected by `docs/omniroute-assessment.md:74`; improve the existing ordered routes and reviewed intelligence policy. |
| Synthetic successes, fabricated fallback bodies, detached retry pretending a failed request succeeded | Rejected at `docs/omniroute-assessment.md:76`; propagate real failure and never replay an ambiguous generation automatically. |
| Unconditional prompt, message, tool or reasoning rewriting | Rejected at `docs/omniroute-assessment.md:78`; only explicit managed features may transform. Raw traffic forwards once. |
| Mandatory single-heavy-model/global admission default | Rejected at `docs/omniroute-assessment.md:81`. A configurable keyed DO admission extension is different and remains Wave 2. |
| Browser-cookie/subscription harvesting, automated rewards and check-ins | Rejected at `docs/omniroute-assessment.md:85`; configured provider keys and authorized native paths are the relevant integration boundary. |
| Semantic generation cache enabled by default | Rejected at `docs/omniroute-assessment.md:87`; approximate answer reuse has weak fit for coding/roleplay. Existing Knowledge semantic memos are a separate capability. |
| SAML/SCIM, recursive tenancy, reseller wallets, invoices and payment processors | Low operator value and large governance burden; Cloudflare Access, existing roles and virtual keys meet current needs. |
| Hedged or speculative paid generations | Doubles potential cost and complicates cancellation, ambiguity and stream ownership; no demonstrated operator need. |
| Local GPU serving, quantization kernels, memory-pressure inference tuning, single-binary rewrite | Poor primary Worker fit; an explicit external provider adapter is cheaper than changing the gateway into a model server. |
| General PII/jailbreak classifiers or remote mandatory safety pipelines | Lower priority than the current secret boundary; significant quality/cost/latency burden. Never describe secret pattern checks as broad PII classification. |
| PQ/sealed two-party audit, timestamp anchoring, key-destruction retention | Interesting tail claims but low near-term value; start with accurate content-free accounting and explicit retention. |
| Always-on generated authenticity/capability health probes | Paid and potentially misleading. Diagnostics stay explicit, bounded and inconclusive unless evidence supports a narrower conclusion. |
| Universal plugin/WASM/Code Mode execution and arbitrary provider-mapping DSL | Large attack and compatibility surface; add narrow reviewed adapters rather than user-authored executable routing rules. |
| Compression by silent hard truncation, unconditional canary prompt insertion, semantic cache warm-up generation | Violates traffic expectations or fidelity unless explicitly opted in; exclude from Wave 1. |
| Live SSE reattachment or unrestricted stored Responses replay | Defer until principal ownership, durable ambiguity and billing settlement exist. Native upstream forwarding remains supported. |

## 6. Wave 1: six independent briefs

Wave 1 selects safety/observability enablers with S/M effort and **disjoint modified and new files**, not simply the six highest scalar scores. Affinity, cache accounting and pool scheduling score highly but share routing/key state and depend on clean outcomes. Six separate worktrees may start from `origin/main` once this report is approved for implementation; this research task creates none.

Shared conventions: defaults preserve existing traffic. New Python tests use `tests/test_*.py`; new Worker tests use `tests/test_*_worker.mjs` and `node --test`. **W5 Prometheus alone owns `package.json`, `worker/container-env.mjs` and the README feature-list edit.** W3's Worker test must be handed to W5/coordinator for registration; W5 also registers its own test. The other briefs supply exact README text in their new docs and must not edit README or package.json. This is a declared integration dependency: each independent branch must keep a runnable script; the coordinator adds W3's filename after its file exists, and checks the final script after merge. W5 also registers the new Flask feature variables in the Container environment allowlist so W1/W4 are activatable on the primary runtime. It does not require shared implementation-file edits.

None of these features needs D1, KV, R2 or DO schema changes. If later work does, use the configured `intelligence-migrations/`, not an invented `worker/migrations/`. Read-only diagnostics must not create provider credentials or change policies.

The owned-file matrix is authoritative. Every feature must not modify any file owned by another feature, including tests and docs. If an unforeseen integration requires overlap, report it to the coordinator rather than taking ownership silently. Common existing test files may be run, but only the listed new test files may be changed.

| Owner | Existing files to modify | New files to add |
| --- | --- | --- |
| W1 | &#96;routes/auto_routes.py&#96; | &#96;services/stream_preflight.py&#96;, &#96;tests/test_stream_preflight.py&#96;, &#96;docs/stream-preflight.md&#96; |

| W6 |  | &#96;scripts/provider_authenticity.py&#96;, &#96;services/provider_authenticity.py&#96;, &#96;tests/test_provider_authenticity.py&#96;, &#96;docs/provider-authenticity.md&#96; |

### W1. Managed automatic Chat stream preflight

**Goal and contract.** Behind `MULTILLM_STREAM_PREFLIGHT=off|strict` (default `off`), delay downstream commitment for streaming `POST /v1/chat/completions` requests whose model is `auto:<route>`. Example request: `{"model":"auto:coding","stream":true,"messages":[{"role":"user","content":"Write a parser"}]}`. In strict mode, a valid first text, reasoning or tool-argument delta unlocks the stream and adds `X-MultiLLM-Stream-Preflight: validated`. Buffered upstream bytes are emitted exactly once, in original order; subsequent bytes are streamed unchanged. Heartbeats, role-only deltas and empty choices do not count as useful output. A terminal empty stream, malformed SSE/JSON envelope or exhausted prefix bound returns a genuine 502 with error code `upstream_stream_invalid`; an upstream read timeout returns 504 `upstream_stream_timeout`. A genuine in-band provider error before useful output returns a failure with sanitized provider error context, never a successful fallback body.

**Runtime.** Both deployments, implemented in Flask because this managed auto Chat endpoint reaches the existing Flask/Container dispatcher. The request forwarding path needs no code change. Container deployment activation requires W5/coordinator's allowlist registration of MULTILLM_STREAM_PREFLIGHT in worker/container-env.mjs; standalone Flask can read the variable directly. Explicit provider/raw/native transports and nonstreaming requests are outside scope. Roleplay already has its own gate.

**Ownership and integration.** Modify only `routes/auto_routes.py`. Add `services/stream_preflight.py`, `tests/test_stream_preflight.py`, `docs/stream-preflight.md`. In `dispatch_auto_route` (`routes/auto_routes.py:147`), preflight immediately after `dispatch_candidate` and before `attempt_outcome` at line 212 or response decoration at 225. Define a bounded `preflight_chat_stream(response, *, max_bytes=65536, max_events=32)` result carrying the real response/iterator and validation outcome. Use Flask's response iterator and close callbacks, not a second upstream request. Existing stream iteration is at `services/upstream_transport.py:24`; bridge translation is lazy at `routes/protocol_bridge.py:137`. Consume the client-facing stream once, retaining byte chunks rather than serializing parsed objects back to SSE.

The gate may alter health attribution to avoid counting a terminal invalid stream as success. It **must not expand `chat_fail_over` replay permission** (`routes/auto_routes.py:58`). A newly generated local 502 must not accidentally enter the existing “provider refused before generation” retry branch: return it terminally or carry an explicit non-replayable outcome through the loop. Empty/malformed output does not prove that the provider generated nothing. Maintain a strict bound on buffered bytes/events and rely on the already configured upstream read timeout; do not promise a hard wall-clock deadline or add an unbounded reader thread.

**Storage.** None. No migration or persisted body.

**Reference.** Read Octopus [`bestruirui/octopus/internal/relay/upstream.go:102`](https://github.com/bestruirui/octopus/blob/d1a26bdd8770a05ee03e73d867c2cf5af9fa2951/internal/relay/upstream.go#L102) for precommit validation. Read MultiLLM `worker/roleplay/stream-validation-gate.mjs:77` for local conventions, without editing it. Copy bounded lookahead and exact replay; avoid importing Octopus retry assumptions or generating substitute assistant content.

**Tests.** `tests/test_stream_preflight.py`: off-mode compatibility; split UTF-8/JSON/SSE boundaries; heartbeat then text; reasoning and streamed tool arguments; role-only/empty terminal output; malformed/in-band error; byte/event limits; timeout; downstream abort; exactly-once close and byte replay; transformed bridge iterator; raw route untouched; an invalid 200 stream cannot trigger a second upstream POST even though the local error is 502. Run this file and existing auto-route/protocol streaming regressions selected with `rg --files tests`. No new Worker test, so no package script edit.

**Docs/README handoff.** New doc gives mode, limits and failure contract. README sentence for W5: “Managed automatic Chat streams can opt into bounded first-event validation before commitment.”

**Acceptance.**
- [ ] Off mode preserves existing dispatch, headers and byte sequence.
- [ ] Strict mode validates only eligible managed automatic Chat streams.
- [ ] Prefix bytes and cleanup run exactly once on success/failure/disconnect.
- [ ] No synthetic success, raw rewriting or newly authorized retry.
- [ ] Route-health success is not recorded solely from an invalid 200 stream.
- [ ] Focused regressions pass; real provider verification is not required for mocked tests.

**Risks / exclusions.** Late errors after commitment remain stream errors. A blocked synchronous read cannot be interrupted by a byte bound; Wave 2 owns shared deadlines. Do not touch proxy_service.py, resilience_service.py, unified.py, protocol_bridge.py, Worker roleplay files, package.json or README.

### W2. Independent replay and health outcome classification

**Goal and contract.** Correct circuit recovery without changing response bytes or retry permissions. A provider 401/403 or caller 400/404/422 must not heal a degraded/open circuit. A 429 increments the existing rate-limit metric and releases an in-flight half-open probe without recording success; ordinary caller errors and authentication rejection release the probe without a provider-outage penalty. Existing provider failure statuses `408,500,502,503,504` retain their penalties. Valid 2xx responses are recovery evidence. All other statuses are neutral unless existing explicit transport policy classifies them differently. No feature flag is necessary for this internal bookkeeping fix: HTTP behavior remains compatible.

**Runtime.** Both deployments through the common Flask provider transport. Native Worker adapters do not share this circuit implementation and must not be expanded in this wave. Existing raw/passthrough circuit bypass remains exact.

**Ownership and integration.** Modify `services/proxy_service.py` and `services/resilience_service.py`. Add `services/upstream_outcome.py`, `tests/test_upstream_outcome.py`, `docs/upstream-outcomes.md`. Put an immutable, dependency-free outcome type in the new module: classification separates `replay_permission`, `provider_health=success|failure|neutral`, `credential_health=accepted|rejected|throttled|unknown` and `reason`. Classification does not itself mutate a pool or authorize retry. Preserve `_should_retry_status` at proxy_service.py:206 and `_should_retry_exception` at 228; preserve connect-vs-ambiguous read distinctions at 214. Integrate provider outcome at `_record_circuit_result` (services/proxy_service.py:256) and `ResilienceService.record_result` (182), replacing the overly broad success branch at 233. Backward-compatible callers of record_result(provider,status,now) must still work.

Credential rejection is a signal, not automatic key deletion or a new global cooldown. Keep existing credential-pool callers and penalties unchanged; explicit integration of model-specific key health belongs to Wave 2. Neutral outcomes must decrement half-open in-flight counts once, must not increment half-open successes, and must not create an infinite permanently occupied probe.

**Storage.** No schema. Existing in-memory/synchronized circuit state uses current fields.

**Reference.** GPTLoad [`tbphp/gpt-load/internal/health/execution_judge.go:98`](https://github.com/tbphp/gpt-load/blob/6c6380ad6b2697e2a04936b830adf98c0abccd46/internal/health/execution_judge.go#L98): copy separation between failure category and execution decision. Avoid copying any “empty response therefore retry” rule or treating request cancellation as evidence of safe replay.

**Tests.** `tests/test_upstream_outcome.py`: table of success/caller/auth/quota/provider statuses; half-open neutral release and no recovery; threshold success/failure behavior; ConnectTimeout vs read timeout/ConnectionError with bytes possibly sent; cancellation neutral; existing method/Idempotency-Key replay restrictions; raw bypass makes one request and no circuit updates; credential signals do not mutate stored secrets/pools. Run the new file and existing resilience/transport tests located in tests. No new Worker test or package edit.

**Docs/README handoff.** Explain the status matrix and why replay permission is separate. README sentence: “Provider circuit recovery distinguishes valid responses, credential rejection, throttling and ambiguous transport failure.”

**Acceptance.**
- [ ] 400/401/403/404/422/429 never close a half-open circuit as success.
- [ ] Neutral outcomes release half-open capacity without outage penalty.
- [ ] Existing 2xx and provider-failure threshold behavior remains tested.
- [ ] Retry count, allowed methods and raw single-forward behavior are unchanged.
- [ ] Credential classification performs no destructive key action.
- [ ] Focused transport and resilience regressions pass.

**Risks / exclusions.** proxy_service.py exceeds 1,000 lines; the safe task-relevant extraction is the new outcome classifier, not a whole-file rewrite. Keep integration edits minimal and report its remaining size. Do not touch auto_routes.py, credential_pool.py, nanogpt_key_pool.py, cost/accounting modules, Worker files, package.json or README.

### W3. Protected per-candidate roleplay context window

**Goal and contract.** Add `ROLEPLAY_CANDIDATE_CONTEXT_REFIT=off|window` (default `off`). In window mode, each eligible roleplay fallback receives a context view fitted to its own known input/output budget instead of losing every smaller-window candidate. Example: a 128k first candidate fails safely, then a 32k candidate receives the same system/directive/latest-user material plus complete recent tool exchanges that fit. The full original conversation remains stored. This is not an API-wide optimizer and never changes raw Chat/Responses/Messages traffic. No new request field is required; existing roleplay request schema stays unchanged.

Expose content-free per-attempt fit metadata through existing trace facts where possible, without editing trace code; otherwise add `X-MultiLLM-Context-Refit: window` and `X-MultiLLM-Context-Refit-Omitted-Groups: <integer>` on a refitted selected response. The current estimated-input header must describe the selected view, not the unrefitted history. If protected material plus output reserve cannot fit any candidate, return the existing 413 `roleplay_context_too_large`.

**Runtime.** Worker only: roleplay generation and persistence live in the session DO; Flask is a facade for this feature.

**Ownership and integration.** Modify `worker/roleplay/endpoint.mjs` and `worker/roleplay/capacity.mjs`. Add `worker/roleplay/candidate-context.mjs`, `tests/test_roleplay_candidate_context_worker.mjs`, `docs/roleplay-candidate-context.md`. Read the new mode directly from this.env.ROLEPLAY_CANDIDATE_CONTEXT_REFIT in endpoint.mjs through a small parser in the new module; do not add it to config.mjs. New pure `prepareCandidateContext` accepts candidate window, messages, requested output reserve, safety margin and the existing token estimator; returns fit/no-fit plus a candidate view, estimate, omission count and reason. Use the existing protection/grouping semantics discovered around endpoint.mjs:229,350 rather than inventing a second memory policy.

The integration is `prepareRoleplayCandidates` (`capacity.mjs:194`) called at endpoint.mjs:516, and the payload factory at 539. In opt-in mode compute bounded candidate plans before dispatch so an unfit candidate is skipped rather than throwing inside `attemptRoleplayCandidates`' payload factory (transport.mjs:925 currently turns such exceptions into terminal errors). Remove only oldest **complete unprotected groups**; preserve system/developer directives, current user/multimodal content, tool-call/result pairs and ordering. Use candidate output reservation and configured safety before calculating fit; unknown windows follow existing behavior. Do not add paid summarization, hard slicing of individual messages or fabricated tool results.

After selection, continuation at endpoint.mjs:582–585, prompt-cache application at 540, estimated usage and response decoration at 609–614 must all use the selected candidate view. Recovery at 534 and durable conversation persistence remain based on full original history. Keep these two message views explicit so fallback windowing cannot silently delete stored history.

**Storage.** None: candidate plans are request-local; existing DO state remains compatible. No migration.

**Reference.** LiteLLM [`BerriAI/litellm/litellm/router_strategy/complexity_router/context_compaction.py:421`](https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/blob/35b0992533ff05b8cab3fe342a1024b4fa6a0ee6/litellm/router_strategy/complexity_router/context_compaction.py#L421) supplies deployment-specific budgeting. Freeway README is inspiration only. Copy the budget boundary, not client-specific state or automatic compression.

**Tests.** `tests/test_roleplay_candidate_context_worker.mjs`, run `node --test tests/test_roleplay_candidate_context_worker.mjs`: off compatibility; smaller fallback now fits; full protected context still fails; complete tool grouping; newest image/user preservation; unknown window; exact boundary/output/safety; no mutation of original arrays; continuation/cache/headers use selected view; durable state and recovery retain originals; no extra generation call. W5/coordinator must append this file to package.json's `test:worker` script. Also run current roleplay compaction/stream/intelligence tests.

**Docs/README handoff.** Describe omission visibility and persistent-history invariants. README sentence: “Roleplay can opt into protected, candidate-specific fallback context windows.”

**Acceptance.**
- [ ] Off behavior is unchanged.
- [ ] Candidate-specific estimates match payloads, cache annotations and continuation.
- [ ] Only complete old unprotected groups are omitted.
- [ ] Stored history/recovery remains complete.
- [ ] Unfit candidates skip cleanly; all-unfit gives the existing 413.
- [ ] New Worker file is registered by W5/coordinator and tests pass.

**Risks / exclusions.** Bound candidate count and input traversal to existing roleplay limits; compute without auxiliary LLM calls. Do not touch roleplay/config.mjs (already oversized), transport.mjs, memory modules, prompt-cache.mjs, turn-trace.mjs, cloudflare-worker.mjs, package.json, README or any neighbour's files.

### W4. Native Anthropic token count with explicit estimate fallback

**Goal and contract.** Keep current `POST /v1/messages/count_tokens` default behavior and `X-MultiLLM-Token-Count: estimate`. Add request header `X-MultiLLM-Token-Count-Mode: estimate|auto|native`; invalid modes return 400. Response body remains `{"input_tokens":123}`. A successful configured native count sets `X-MultiLLM-Token-Count: provider`; `auto` may fall back to the local estimator only for an unsupported/unconfigured capability, reporting `X-MultiLLM-Token-Count-Fallback: unsupported`. `native` with unsupported capability returns 501 using the existing Anthropic error-envelope helper, with a message identifying unsupported native counting. Upstream auth/permission/rate-limit/server errors, timeout or malformed count return genuine protocol errors in both auto/native modes; no silent fallback that hides these failures.

Configure only operator-verified endpoints with `NATIVE_TOKEN_COUNT_ENDPOINTS_JSON` (default `{}`). Shape: `{"provider-id":"/v1/messages/count_tokens"}`. Entries are registered provider IDs and origin-absolute paths ending in `/messages/count_tokens` on the provider's existing trusted origin. Require a single leading slash; reject schemes, `//`, dot segments, query and fragment. Build from the trusted base URL's scheme/host and configured exact path, so a base already containing `/v1` is not duplicated; an operator must include any `/api` or `/coding` prefix explicitly. Do **not** assume that a provider supports native counting because its Messages endpoint works; this study has not live-verified any listed reseller's count endpoint. Only explicit `provider:model` identifiers are eligible initially; auto/bare aliases retain estimate in auto mode and are unsupported in native mode. Authorize the original provider-qualified model, then send its parsed provider-local model string. Native payload retains system, messages, tools and supported count fields; it must never send a generation request disguised as `max_tokens=1`.

**Runtime.** Both: Flask owns this route and the Worker already forwards the same path to the Container. No native Worker duplicate or new request forwarding branch. W5/coordinator must register NATIVE_TOKEN_COUNT_ENDPOINTS_JSON in worker/container-env.mjs before configuring this in a Worker-hosted Container.

**Ownership and integration.** Modify only `routes/unified_messages.py`. Add `services/native_token_count.py`, `tests/test_native_token_count.py`, `docs/token-counting.md`. Integrate in `unified_messages_count_tokens` at lines 87–93; retain the existing estimator at 57. Preserve auth decorator `api_authenticate_only`, then enforce requested-model access with `services/key_controls.py:model_allowed` (202) and `g.authenticated_user` before any native provider call. This prevents the count route from bypassing key model restrictions. Use `ModelRegistry.parse_model_id` (`services/model_registry.py:105`) and the registered trusted provider configuration.

Use existing `AuthService.get_api_key` (`services/auth_service.py:673`) and `ProxyService.prepare_headers` (services/proxy_service.py:393) with upstream_path set to the configured count path for configured credentials. A single bounded request may use `ProxyService._get_provider_session(provider, raw_passthrough=True)` (`services/proxy_service.py:146`), whose adapter has zero automatic retries and suppresses cookies. Send count-only POST with redirects disabled and timeout (5,10), bounded JSON response (64 KiB), and no fallback provider. Do not call the general generation retry machinery. Validate a nonnegative integer count, reject bool/string/negative/missing/nonfinite/oversized bodies, and release upstream response exactly once. Unknown incoming vendor fields follow the count adapter allowlist, not foreign protocol extras.

**Storage.** None; no count cache, price charge, token reservation or schema change.

**Reference.** OmniRoute [`diegosouzapw/OmniRoute/src/app/api/v1/messages/count_tokens/route.ts:92`](https://github.com/diegosouzapw/OmniRoute/blob/3def9ae3105035fa0e7605a7d6842139d3213cae/src/app/api/v1/messages/count_tokens/route.ts#L92) illustrates native-count validation and explicit estimation. Copy plausibility/error labeling; avoid browser/subscription auth and inferring native capability from estimates.

**Tests.** `tests/test_native_token_count.py`: default estimate unchanged; auth/model denial makes no upstream call; native success preserves relevant payload; unsupported native/auto contracts; malformed config/provider/path rejected safely; no duplicated /v1 with prefixed base URLs; model prefix stripped only after authorization; no redirects/retries; timeout; 401/403/429/5xx do not become estimated successes; invalid count forms; response bound/cleanup; tools/system/multimodal estimation compatibility. Run new file and existing protocol-route tests. No new Worker test/script edit.

**Docs/README handoff.** Show header modes, endpoint allowlist and unsupported behavior without promising any reseller capability. README sentence: “Messages token counting can use explicitly configured native provider endpoints with labeled local estimates.”

**Acceptance.**
- [ ] Default estimator/body/header contract is preserved.
- [ ] Key/model authorization precedes native dispatch.
- [ ] Exactly one count request, no generation or provider failover.
- [ ] Count source is visible and failures are honest.
- [ ] Unsupported auto fallback is explicit; upstream failures do not disappear.
- [ ] Mocked route/protocol tests pass.

**Risks / exclusions.** Endpoint availability requires operator verification later. Do not edit provider credential data, config.py, provider registry/protocol declarations, proxy_service.py, unified.py, cloudflare-worker.mjs, package.json or README.

### W5. Bounded Prometheus exposition and shared registration

**Goal and contract.** Add `PROMETHEUS_ENABLED=false` by default. Enabled deployments expose two deliberate scopes:
1. Worker `GET|HEAD /status.prometheus`: public, only the already public route-health snapshot, with no Container wake.
2. Flask/Worker-forwarded `GET /v1/metrics/prometheus`: detailed content-free operator metrics, existing authenticated API key with admin scope **and** admin user, 401/403 otherwise. It may wake the Container because it requests detailed Flask metrics.

Disabled paths return 404. Enabled unsupported methods return 405 with Allow; existing API OPTIONS handling remains compatible. Format `Content-Type: text/plain; version=0.0.4; charset=utf-8`; detailed response `Cache-Control: no-store`. No new scrape secret or unauthenticated detailed metric. Public surface is safe because /status.json already publishes the same health facts.

Metric names: `multillm_status_state{scope="overall|route|provider",name="<bounded public name>",state="up|degraded|down|unknown"}` one-hot gauges preserving the public snapshot states at worker/status-page.mjs:13; unrecognized states map to unknown. Down is not asserted to mean an open circuit. `multillm_status_snapshot_available` is a 0/1 gauge. `multillm_status_snapshot_age_seconds` exists only for a retained/live snapshot with a valid generated_at timestamp, never for the fallback emptySnapshot timestamp. Detailed `multillm_requests_window{window="24h",status_class="2xx|3xx|4xx|5xx|other"}` and `multillm_latency_window_seconds{window="24h",quantile="0.50|0.95"}` are **gauges**, not counters or histograms. Use existing `MetricsService.get_stats(hours=24)`; milliseconds are proven by metrics_service.py:228 and core.py:699, so divide latency by 1000. Omit latency quantiles when there are no observations. Expose `multillm_observed_requests_window` and a content-free coverage label `source="flask_ledger"`; this scrape does not measure all native edge requests. Do not emit fabricated cost, TTFT or provider-billed totals.

**Runtime.** Both, split as above. No long-lived exporter process or third-party metrics dependency.

**Ownership and integration.** Modify `cloudflare-worker.mjs`, `worker/status-page.mjs`, `worker/container-env.mjs`, `routes/core.py`, `package.json`, `README.md`. Add `worker/prometheus.mjs`, `services/prometheus_export.py`, `tests/test_prometheus_worker.mjs`, `tests/test_prometheus.py`, `docs/prometheus.md`. Export/rename the meaningful existing loader `loadSnapshot` as `loadStatusSnapshot` (`worker/status-page.mjs:151`) and update its internal caller; share its D1-first/fetchIfRunning/unknown behavior rather than duplicating snapshot access. Extend the internal loader result with available=true for retained/live data and available=false for emptySnapshot; the existing status handler ignores this internal field, preserving its JSON/HTML contract. Register one narrow Worker import/dispatch branch beside status handling (`cloudflare-worker.mjs:1880`), before generic Container dispatch at 2040. Disabled/public metric paths must not fall through and wake the Container. Keep HEAD/body/cache behavior tested.

In `register_core_routes` (routes/core.py:196), add the detailed API route near admin metrics at core.py:645. Import Flask `g` and the existing API auth decorator; use `api_authenticate_only(required_scope="admin")` as demonstrated by routes/status_page.py:96, plus `g.authenticated_user.get("is_admin")`. Do not reuse `require_admin_dashboard_user` (159), which reads browser session identity rather than API key identity. Read MetricsService without modifying metric collection or the ledger. Pure serializers escape backslash, quotes and newlines in labels; reject nonfinite values; emit HELP/TYPE; cap 512 total series and 256 KiB output with deterministic truncation and an explicit truncation gauge. Never label by prompt, key/prefix, principal, URL, request ID or arbitrary user model strings. Public names come from the bounded sanitized status snapshot.

This feature owns the shared registration changes. In its independent branch append only its own existing tests/test_prometheus_worker.mjs to package.json:test:worker (line 12), so that branch's test script stays runnable. After W3 is integrated, W5/coordinator appends tests/test_roleplay_candidate_context_worker.mjs in the integration commit; W3 does not edit the script. Add this feature's README sentence in its branch; the remaining five sentences are coordinator/W5 integration edits after their features exist. Register MULTILLM_STREAM_PREFLIGHT, NATIVE_TOKEN_COUNT_ENDPOINTS_JSON and PROMETHEUS_ENABLED in DIRECT_ENV_KEYS (worker/container-env.mjs:1), since shouldPassThroughKey at worker/container-env.mjs:188 currently drops unknown variables and MultiLLMProxyContainer at cloudflare-worker.mjs:1806 uses collectContainerEnv. These entries are harmless before other branches merge. Add a fixture assertion to this feature's new Node test proving each setting passes to the Container with no credential values printed. No broad wildcard env passthrough.

**Reference.** LiteLLM [`BerriAI/litellm/litellm/integrations/prometheus_metrics_endpoint.py:67`](https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/blob/35b0992533ff05b8cab3fe342a1024b4fa6a0ee6/litellm/integrations/prometheus_metrics_endpoint.py#L67): copy exposition conventions, not its worker threads or library dependency. ### W6. Explicit provider authenticity and conformance diagnostics

**Goal and contract.** Supply an operator CLI for deployed Worker or Flask gateways, with content-free JSON observations rather than claiming to identify a hidden model. Example:
`python scripts/provider_authenticity.py --base-url https://gateway.example --model nanogpt:example-model`.
Default performs one metadata request, no generation, and normally returns `{"verdict":"unknown","generation_requests":0,"coverage":["catalog_claim"],"limitations":["identity_not_verified"]}`. Set `--allow-generation` explicitly for a bounded synthetic conformance probe. Total request budget is three (one models request and at most two Chat requests), generation max_tokens 64 each, timeout 10 seconds per request, no retries or redirects. Print a pre-dispatch plan with request count and token caps; when provider price is unknown, projected cost is null, not zero.

Signals: model catalog compatibility, response protocol/model claims, schema correctness, usage plausibility, independent nonce handling and repeated envelope consistency. Useful optional tokenizer-delta probes are **deferred** until W4 native counting is available and explicitly selected; do not pretend the current estimator supplies model-family evidence. A returned model name or self-identification answer contributes only a claim. Verdicts are `supported|contradicted|unknown`, with a `claim` field (for example `"chat_protocol_conformance"`); `supported` can apply to observed conformance, never cryptographic model identity. Contradictions must name the narrow violated contract and evidence coverage.

**Runtime.** Both as external operator diagnostics targeting their ordinary gateway HTTP endpoints. There is no hot-path Worker/Flask handler, scheduled probe, automatic routing change or dashboard change.

**Ownership and integration.** Add only `scripts/provider_authenticity.py`, `services/provider_authenticity.py`, `tests/test_provider_authenticity.py`, `docs/provider-authenticity.md`. The service module is a pure signal evaluator plus an injectable bounded HTTP collaborator; CLI owns arguments, private auth loading and output. Read an existing operator key only from `MULTILLM_API_KEY` privately; no key argument, .env reading, credential printing or saved auth headers. Use existing requests dependency if available, not a new SDK. Reject base URLs with userinfo, query or fragment, and require HTTPS except explicitly allowed loopback HTTP for local tests. Use existing `/v1/models` and `/v1/chat/completions`; require an explicit provider-qualified model, reject `auto:*` so one probe does not fan out. Preserve normal gateway model entitlement enforcement.

Synthetic nonce prompts are generated by the tool; output omits raw prompt/completion. Store only request indices, observed schema/usage fields, boolean nonce results, hashes and limitations. Report no paid/billed assertion without provider evidence. Do not catch exceptions as successful verdicts; metadata errors or insufficient probes yield unknown with a safe reason. Exit 0 for supported observed conformance, 2 for contradiction, 3 for inconclusive/insufficient evidence, 1 for CLI/config errors. Use injected clients for offline tests.

**Storage.** None. JSON goes to stdout; optional shell redirection is operator-controlled. No new key file, database, receipt signature, persistent provider score or migration.

**Reference.** Casbin [`apache/casbin-gateway/object/provider_probe_checks.go:47`](https://github.com/apache/casbin-gateway/blob/804f191594de4cde0e9d2a84ed884e42de1979eb/object/provider_probe_checks.go#L47) and `object/provider_probe_score.go:27` for independent observations. The latter path is under the same clone directory. Copy signal separation; avoid turning a heuristic score, response.model or tokenizer match into proof of identity. Existing MultiLLM catalog-only discovery `services/provider_capability_discovery.py:328` and free checks `services/health_checks.py:1` remain unchanged.

**Tests.** `tests/test_provider_authenticity.py`: default zero generation; explicit plan/caps; metadata-only inconclusive; spoofed model string not proof; valid/invalid envelopes; usage unknown/partial; nonce contradictions; no retries/redirects; timeout/error; unsupported auto/model URL; loopback opt-in; finite request budget; credential/prompt redaction on every exception and JSON output; no automatic provider/config write. Tests use deterministic fake transports, never live credentials/network.

**Docs/README handoff.** Explain narrow verdicts, explicit generation cost, request caps and inconclusive evidence. README sentence: “Operator diagnostics provide bounded, content-free provider conformance and authenticity signals.”

**Acceptance.**
- [ ] Default issues no generation.
- [ ] Explicit generation has a visible finite request/token budget.
- [ ] Model claims cannot produce a verified-identity verdict.
- [ ] Outputs retain uncertainty and contain no credentials or raw user content.
- [ ] No changes to live routing, credential health or stored capability policies.
- [ ] Offline fake-transport tests pass; live probes are separately authorized operator work.

**Risks / exclusions.** Identical metadata and heuristic token signatures cannot distinguish all reseller models. Do not add automatic spending, background probes or subjective model-quality comparisons. Must not touch existing scripts, provider/discovery/pool modules, config.py, routes, Worker files, package.json or README.


## 7. Wave 2 candidates

**Prompt-cache-aware outcome affinity (92.4, M, both).** Use stable principal/session plus safe prefix digest to prefer a successful provider/credential within the existing eligible chain. Existing roleplay stickiness and OpenCode headers are narrower precedents. Define affinity TTL, config revision, failure eviction and hash-only identity; never expose key material. Measure cache-read savings and count the cost of cold rerouting before adding any dynamic preference. Needs W2 outcomes and overlaps credential/routing files; hence deferred despite its highest scalar score.

**Cache policy isolation (83.6, M, both).** Add route and guardrail policy revision to Chat exact-cache identity, with canonical payload hashing and conservative eligible endpoints. Knowledge identity already has richer scoping. On config change old entries become unreachable through a new key rather than being destructively purged. Keep principal isolation, no stored credential identity and existing cache opt-in behavior.

**Provider Retry-After/reset hints (82.6, S, both).** Parse bounded delta-seconds and HTTP-date once, clamp to an operator limit and expose an explicit advice source. Reuse free-pool reset handling without sleeping past a shared request deadline. A wait hint does not authorize a second POST. Preserve raw headers/one-forward behavior and distinguish throttling from hard account exhaustion.

**Soonest-reset account scheduling (81.6, M, both).** Select an available key inside a configured pool using fresh quota/reset observations, stable ties and the current fallback priority. Unknown/stale reset facts revert to existing rotation. Persist only opaque account IDs and normalized availability, not raw keys; do not import browser/subscription harvesting or an alternative strategy catalog.

**Typed partial usage/cost (81.2, S, both).** Preserve null separately for each missing input/output/cache usage field, and expose price availability plus measured/estimated/unknown basis. Existing whole-usage null and unknown-price handling stays intact. Tests must show missing output is not zero and unknown cost is not free. This is a representation correction, not an invoice engine; review any downstream schema assumptions before changing storage.

**Shared keyed concurrency admission (80.7, L, both).** A bounded DO lease per virtual key/model group must cover native Worker dispatch and forwarded Flask requests, with renewal/expiry and exactly-once release on rejection/abort. Avoid a fixed global single-heavy-model default. This is the one deliberately L-sized next-wave item: prototype admission/accounting ownership first, then split a safe implementation; no Redis/process-local semaphore pretending to be global.

**End-to-end generation deadline (80.7, M, both).** Propagate one monotonic remaining budget across allowed attempts, token-count-independent setup, first-event wait and stream cancellation. Cascade/intelligence local budgets are precedents. Deadline expiry returns a real timeout and an uncertain spend outcome if provider handoff occurred; it never licenses a fallback after an ambiguous generation. Validate cancellation and half-open cleanup with W1/W2.

**Model-scoped credential cooldown (80.6, M, both).** Store throttle/exhaustion state by opaque credential and model or documented shared quota bucket, not blanket provider key. Reset from provider evidence, retain auth-wide rejection when genuinely credential-wide, and integrate W2 categories. Ensure NanoGPT/free pool/provider session distinctions do not accidentally broaden request replay.

**Prompt-cache usage and price buckets (80.4, M, both).** Capture ordinary input, cache-read input, cache-write input and output separately from real provider usage. Use model-specific price metadata with null for unknown components; expose a cost basis and provenance. Native protocol translators may already carry cache usage fields, but flat cost functions understate warm/cold differences. No assumption that a reseller's catalog price equals its billed amount.

**Conversion-loss diagnostics (78.5, M, both).** Add an explicit field-level report with exact/semantic/lossy/unsupported categories around managed bridges. Use header/dry-run metadata with no prompt content; default behavior and strict failures stay unchanged. Source-protocol extra namespaces remain a later extension, not a backdoor for sending unknown fields to unrelated providers.

Broader native-edge metrics, honest unresolved budget holds, zero-content retention, MCP compatibility digests and disconnect cancellation follow these foundations. They are still ranked, but are not folded into a supposedly small first-wave feature.

These are the actual independent top-40 rg searches, case-insensitive with source globs *.py, *.mjs, *.sql and *.json. Positive results were read around the relevant code before assigning partial status. A zero result is scoped absence evidence; it is not proof about provider services or deployments. Signed-receipt search has one incidental credential-path detector, which was excluded. Broad positive searches establish existing partial behavior; the ranked table explains the missing extension.
